| [registers.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/registers.rs), `measure` | Sums branch weights into T, forms `-u*T`, and selects only when the exact sign of cumulative weight minus uT is `Greater`. Consumes the register after selection. |
| [exact/measurement.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/exact/measurement.rs), `real_sign` | Decides exact zero before refining outward enclosures; equality is not resolved with a tolerance or binary64 display probability. |
| [classical.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/classical.rs), `measurement_draw`, `transaction`, measure row | Pops the front of the queue; otherwise advances its measurement PRNG. Failed transactional steps restore queue and PRNG as well as state. Every successful measurement obtains a draw, even for a basis state. |
| [main.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/main.rs), options and `measurements_json` | Parses each `--draws` item as binary64, validates through `push_measurement_draw`, and prints wire-first bits and the little-endian integer. |
| [SPEC-QUESTIONS 29](../../../reference-interpreter/SPEC-QUESTIONS.md), [README](../../../reference-interpreter/README.md) | Documents this CLI convention for the interpreter's **0.8** execution surface. It is not evidence that this binary admits 0.9 objects. |

Let the measured register's wires be b0,...,b(n−1) in register order. For
each outcome s, let P_s project on those bits while leaving every other
live wire alone, and let w_s = ||P_s ψ||². Enumerate all bit strings s in
lexicographic order with b0 printed first, 0 before 1. Set T = Σ_s w_s > 0,
C_j = Σ_(i≤j) w_(s_i), C_−1 = 0. For a supplied real draw 0 ≤ u < 1:

```text
selected index = min { j : C_j > u*T }
selected outcome s_j iff C_(j-1)/T <= u < C_j/T
returned value = sum_(i=0..n-1) s_j[i] * 2^i
conditional residual state = P_(s_j) ψ / sqrt(w_(s_j)), with measured wires removed
```

Zero-weight outcomes have empty intervals and are never selected. At any
equality `u*T=C_j`, skip that outcome and any following zeros, selecting
the next positive weight. At u=0 choose the first supported outcome; u=1
is outside the domain. For n=2 the strings `00,01,10,11` have integer
values **0,2,1,3**, not 0,1,2,3. Sorting by the returned integer gives a
different coupling to supplied draws. A multi-qubit measurement consumes
**one** draw for its joint outcome, not one draw per qubit.

After a measurement, compute subsequent weights from the conditional
state. The exact shadow may be unnormalized, so compare against uT rather
than presuming T=1. An independent two-wire |++> followed by separate
measurements at draws 0.25,0.5 returns 0,1 even when the first projection
leaves the exact shadow with total weight 1/2. Entangled wires retain the
correct conditional correlations; a Bell pair measured separately with
0.5,0 returns 1,1.

The interval length is w_s/T, exactly the Born probability. Choosing a
half-open endpoint convention changes only the deterministic coupling to
a supplied draw; it preserves the Born law in §§4.1/14.2. It does not
change gates, coherent control, oracle faults, register ownership, the
measurement ban inside unitary regions, or source measurement's reversal
bar. No North Star capability is narrowed: classical feedback, repeated
measurements, arbitrary-width registers and all demo rungs remain in scope.

## Draw streams, seeds and CLI boundaries

Draws are consumed in **dynamic execution order**, across calls, branches
and loops, one per successfully executed measure. Untaken rows consume
none; deterministic successful measurements consume one; a failed
precondition consumes none. Multiple qubits in one measure still consume
one. There is no independent queue per register or function. Validation of
a supplied draw list is before execution, including any surplus entries.

The CLI's decimal token is parsed to binary64 first. Its exact dyadic
value is the u used by the exact comparison. Thus
`0.49999999999999999` rounds to 0.5 and returns 1 for H, whereas the
round-trip spelling `0.49999999999999994` denotes the predecessor of 0.5
and returns 0. A decimal-looking near tie is not necessarily a different
binary64 draw. Both signs of zero denote the endpoint 0. Nonfinite values,
negative values other than negative zero, and 1 or greater reject. Repeated
`--draws` options reject; a comma-separated list is one option.

When the queue is exhausted the current reference uses xoshiro256** state
`[1,2,3,4]`, taking `(next_u64 >> 11)/2^53`. Supplied draws do not advance
that generator. Surplus valid draws are unused, without a leftover-list
diagnostic. These are reproduced/source-derived **CLI** conventions, not
new universal ISA PRNG/seed requirements. An exhausted list still gets a
Born draw under 0.9; do not mandate an exhaustion fault or a zero fallback.
`--entropy` belongs to the effect device and does not seed measurements.
`--seed` is accepted by `step-check` for §12.1 sampling, not by `run-cc`
or `run-program`. Do not equate that sampler with measurement randomness.
A future execution API may expose a seed; replay portability requires it
to state generator, state and number conversion, or supply an explicit
common draw stream. K17 fixes mapping/consumption, not a cross-executor
unsupplied-random-bit identity. No new seeded CLI is proposed.

## Conformance vectors and consumer impact

All draws in this table have exact binary64 representations except the
explicit parsing vector. Decimal spellings use round-trip values.

| State / measurements | Draw(s) | Expected wire-first bits; integer(s) |
|---|---|---|
| H\|0> | 0 | `0`; 0 |
| H\|0> | 0.49999999999999994 | `0`; 0 |
| H\|0> | 0.5 | `1`; 1 |
| H\|0> | 0.5000000000000001 | `1`; 1 |
| H\|0> | 0.9999999999999999 | `1`; 1 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.24999999999999997 / 0.25 / 0.25000000000000006 | `00`/`01`/`01`; 0/2/2 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.49999999999999994 / 0.5 / 0.5000000000000001 | `01`/`10`/`10`; 2/1/1 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.7499999999999999 / 0.75 / 0.7500000000000001 | `10`/`11`/`11`; 1/3/3 |
| Two fresh \|++> registers, joint measures | 0.25,0.75 | `01`,`11`; 2,3 (published K17 carrier) |
| Fresh deterministic 0, then fresh H\|0> | 0.75,0.25 | `0`,`0`; 0,0 |
| Independent \|++>, measured one wire at a time | 0.25,0.5 | `0`,`1`; 0,1 (conditional normalization) |
| Bell pair, joint | 0.5 | `11`; 3 (unsupported outcomes skipped) |
| Bell pair, separate wires | 0.5,0 | `1`,`1`; 1,1 |
| H\|0>, decimal parsing witness | token 0.49999999999999999 | `1`; 1 after binary64 conversion |

Further acceptance vectors: a repeated call/loop with asymmetric draws;
a branch that skips a measurement; a width/view precondition failure
followed by retry through an API that permits retry, verifying no queue or
PRNG advancement; 128-bit/high-wire values and run-time-width 0.9 measures;
and reversal rejection after any successful source measure. These are
**proposed**, not executions of 0.9 or fault-injection coverage here.

For bennett-vm use strict `CDF > u*T`, wire-first outcome ordering and
conditional weights; retain the stated VM fix if it meets those rules.
Floating roundoff with an epsilon tie test is not this rule. For the
conformance harness serialize explicit common draws, compare bits and
little-endian values, and test FIFO separately from threshold strictness.
Do not mask the H disagreement by dropping exact ties or use two executors'
seed defaults as a portable oracle. 0.8 draws are an explicitly documented
reference/harness profile; 0.9 draws are normative K17. Existing broad
harness claims require their owner's rerun; these finite vectors do not
establish all 32 cases.

```python
from pathlib import Path
import json, math, os, subprocess
ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-isa-textdefects'
BIN = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
def cli(*args, good=True):
    p = subprocess.run([str(BIN), *map(str, args)], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=15)
    if good:
        assert p.returncode == 0, (args, p.stderr)
        return json.loads(p.stdout)
    assert p.returncode == 1 and not p.stdout and p.stderr, (args, p)
    return p.stderr

def fixture(name, actions, cc, results):
    symbols, bodies = {}, []
    for i, (n, widths, body) in enumerate(actions):
        sig = dict(abi='0.8',args=[],bounds=[],**{'in':[]},kind='action',mem=[],mut=widths,tier='q')
        params = ', '.join(f'q{j}:wire[{w}] mut' for j,w in enumerate(widths))
        symbols[str(i)] = dict(interface_hash='0'*64,kind='action',name=n,signature=sig)
        bodies.append(f'action @{n} ({params}) () () () ()\n^entry():\n{body}\n')
    sig = dict(abi='0.8',kind='cc',params=[],results=[f'i{w}' for w in results],tier='cc')
    symbols[str(len(symbols))] = dict(interface_hash='0'*64,kind='cc',name='main',signature=sig)
    outs = ', '.join(f'r{i}:i{w}' for i,w in enumerate(results))
    bodies.append(f'fn cc @main () ({outs}) () () ()\n^entry():\n{cc}\nret '+ ' '.join(f'r{i}' for i in range(len(results)))+'\n')
    def text():
        return ('bennett-object isa=0.8 object=0.8 launch=0.8 sched=0.8 cert=0.8 coverage=0.8 target=0.8 ptr_width=32\n\nsection symbols\n'+json.dumps(symbols,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':'))+'\n\nsection functions\n'+''.join(bodies))
    path = JOB / (name+'.bisa')
    path.write_text(text())
    hashes = cli('hash', path)['symbols']
    for sym in symbols.values():
        sym['interface_hash'] = hashes[sym['name']]['interface_hash']
    path.write_text(text())
    assert cli('check', path)['status'] == 'ok'
    return path
h1 = [('prep',[1],'h q0[0]')]
one = fixture('one-h', h1, 'q = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[1])
two = fixture('two-h', [('prep',[2],'h q0[0]\nh q0[1]')], 'q = qreg.new any 2\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[2])
bell = fixture('bell', [('prep',[2],'h q0[0]\ncx q0[0] q0[1]')], 'q = qreg.new any 2\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[2])
det = fixture('deterministic', [], 'q = qreg.new any 1\nr0 = measure q',[1])
fifo = fixture('det-then-h', h1, 'a = qreg.new any 1\nr0 = measure a\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (b) () () ()\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
conditional = fixture('conditional', [('prep',[1,1],'h q0[0]\nh q1[0]')], 'a = qreg.new any 1\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (a, b) () () ()\nr0 = measure a\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
entangled = fixture('entangled', [('prep',[1,1],'h q0[0]\ncx q0[0] q1[0]')], 'a = qreg.new any 1\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (a, b) () () ()\nr0 = measure a\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
checks = []
def check(path, draws, bits, values, command='run-cc'):
    actual=cli(command,path,'main','--draws',draws)
    m=actual['measurements']
    assert [x['bits'] for x in m] == bits, (path.name,draws,m,bits)
    assert [int(x['value']) for x in m] == values
    checks.append(dict(fixture=path.name,draws=draws,bits=bits,values=values,command=command))
    return actual
for u,want in [(0.,0),(math.nextafter(.5,0),0),(.5,1),(math.nextafter(.5,1),1),(math.nextafter(1.,0),1)]:
    check(one,repr(u),[str(want)],[want])
for j,u in enumerate([0.,.25,.5,.75]):
    for d,k in [(u,j),(math.nextafter(u,1),j)]+([] if j==0 else [(math.nextafter(u,0),j-1)]):
        bits=['00','01','10','11'][k]
        check(two,repr(d),[bits],[[0,2,1,3][k]])
check(two,repr(math.nextafter(1.,0)),['11'],[3])
check(bell,'0.5',['11'],[3])
check(det,'0.9999999999999999',['0'],[0])
check(fifo,'0.75,0.25',['0','0'],[0,0])
check(conditional,'0.25,0.5',['0','1'],[0,1])
check(entangled,'0.5,0',['1','1'],[1,1])
check(one,'0.49999999999999999',['1'],[1]) # binary64 parsing rounds to 1/2
check(one,'-0',['0'],[0])
check(one,'0.5,0.1',['1'],[1]) # surplus draws do not reject
check(one,'0.5',['1'],[1],command='run-program')
# Default PRNG starts at [1,2,3,4]; these first draws are below 1/2.
check(one,'',['0'],[0])
check(fifo,'0.75',['0','0'],[0,0])
# Published K17's executable carrier is a 0.8 witness, not 0.9 admission.
carrier=ROOT/'conformance/cases/A/A-measure-draw-order-09/two-measures.bisa'
m=cli('run-program',carrier,'twice','--draws','0.25,0.75')['measurements']
assert [(x['bits'],int(x['value'])) for x in m] == [('01',2),('11',3)]
for invalid in ['-0.1','1','NaN','inf','0.5,NaN']:
    error=cli('run-cc',one,'main','--draws',invalid,good=False)
    assert '[0,1)' in error, error
assert 'seed' in cli('run-cc',one,'main','--seed','1,2,3,4',good=False)
assert 'twice' in cli('run-cc',one,'main','--draws','0.5','--draws','0.5',good=False)
(JOB/'draw-results.json').write_text(json.dumps(checks,indent=2)+'\n')
print(f'PASS: {len(checks)} draw vectors; published K17 carrier; 7 CLI rejection vectors')
```

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 0h4 — measurement draws, exact ties and the existing K17 rule

Council derivation for `b-isa-spec-0h4`, related `b-isa-spec-bas`.
Proposal and reproduced evidence only; no spec, catalog or interpreter edit.

## Finding: the checked-in 0.9 already decides this

The advisor's 2026-11-02T07:55Z witness is reproducible: H on a fresh wire,
then `--draws 0.5`, returns **1** in the reference interpreter. The supplied
report of the pre-fix VM returning 0 is historical evidence; this job did
not run either VM revision or reproduce the reported 24/32 harness tally.

The claim that **neither** published text states the tie rule is stale for
this checkout. [0.8 §4.1](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec.md#41-classical-control-cc-r)
only names the Born rule. [0.9 §4.1](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md#41-classical-control-cc-r)
already says “strictly greater”, explicitly sends a CDF tie to the next
nonzero-weight outcome, specifies FIFO including deterministic measurements,
and includes two exact ties. Its §0.2 K17 and “Decided confirmations” record
ratification; `bd show b-isa-spec-bas` also reports the applied rule. This
is evidence against reintroducing an absent-text defect in current 0.9,
not a claim that the VM has always implemented K17. A consumer pinned to
another snapshot should compare the actual §4.1 bytes before diagnosis.

Recommendation: retain K17, optionally apply the editorial strengthening
below, add the single-H boundary vectors to the harness, and disposition
0h4 after council reviews this evidence. Keep the issue open here: notes
alone do not accept a council change. Do not reopen accepted semantics or
change the catalog to compensate for a consumer comparator.

## Derivation from the reference

Read-only implementation evidence:

| Source | Determining behavior |
|---|---|
| [quantum.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/quantum.rs), `measurement_branches` | Projects jointly on the requested wire list. `BTreeMap<Vec<bool>,...>` orders bits lexicographically, `false < true`. Sums squared exact amplitudes within each projection; retains the conditional state of other wires. |
| [registers.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/registers.rs), `measure` | Sums branch weights into T, forms `-u*T`, and selects only when the exact sign of cumulative weight minus uT is `Greater`. Consumes the register after selection. |
| [exact/measurement.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/exact/measurement.rs), `real_sign` | Decides exact zero before refining outward enclosures; equality is not resolved with a tolerance or binary64 display probability. |
| [classical.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/classical.rs), `measurement_draw`, `transaction`, measure row | Pops the front of the queue; otherwise advances its measurement PRNG. Failed transactional steps restore queue and PRNG as well as state. Every successful measurement obtains a draw, even for a basis state. |
| [main.rs](../../../reference-interpreter/src/main.rs), options and `measurements_json` | Parses each `--draws` item as binary64, validates through `push_measurement_draw`, and prints wire-first bits and the little-endian integer. |
| [SPEC-QUESTIONS 29](../../../reference-interpreter/SPEC-QUESTIONS.md), [README](../../../reference-interpreter/README.md) | Documents this CLI convention for the interpreter's **0.8** execution surface. It is not evidence that this binary admits 0.9 objects. |

Let the measured register's wires be b0,...,b(n−1) in register order. For
each outcome s, let P_s project on those bits while leaving every other
live wire alone, and let w_s = ||P_s ψ||². Enumerate all bit strings s in
lexicographic order with b0 printed first, 0 before 1. Set T = Σ_s w_s > 0,
C_j = Σ_(i≤j) w_(s_i), C_−1 = 0. For a supplied real draw 0 ≤ u < 1:

```text
selected index = min { j : C_j > u*T }
selected outcome s_j iff C_(j-1)/T <= u < C_j/T
returned value = sum_(i=0..n-1) s_j[i] * 2^i
conditional residual state = P_(s_j) ψ / sqrt(w_(s_j)), with measured wires removed
```

Zero-weight outcomes have empty intervals and are never selected. At any
equality `u*T=C_j`, skip that outcome and any following zeros, selecting
the next positive weight. At u=0 choose the first supported outcome; u=1
is outside the domain. For n=2 the strings `00,01,10,11` have integer
values **0,2,1,3**, not 0,1,2,3. Sorting by the returned integer gives a
different coupling to supplied draws. A multi-qubit measurement consumes
**one** draw for its joint outcome, not one draw per qubit.

After a measurement, compute subsequent weights from the conditional
state. The exact shadow may be unnormalized, so compare against uT rather
than presuming T=1. An independent two-wire |++> followed by separate
measurements at draws 0.25,0.5 returns 0,1 even when the first projection
leaves the exact shadow with total weight 1/2. Entangled wires retain the
correct conditional correlations; a Bell pair measured separately with
0.5,0 returns 1,1.

The interval length is w_s/T, exactly the Born probability. Choosing a
half-open endpoint convention changes only the deterministic coupling to
a supplied draw; it preserves the Born law in §§4.1/14.2. It does not
change gates, coherent control, oracle faults, register ownership, the
measurement ban inside unitary regions, or source measurement's reversal
bar. No North Star capability is narrowed: classical feedback, repeated
measurements, arbitrary-width registers and all demo rungs remain in scope.

## Draw streams, seeds and CLI boundaries

Draws are consumed in **dynamic execution order**, across calls, branches
and loops, one per successfully executed measure. Untaken rows consume
none; deterministic successful measurements consume one; a failed
precondition consumes none. Multiple qubits in one measure still consume
one. There is no independent queue per register or function. Validation of
a supplied draw list is before execution, including any surplus entries.

The CLI's decimal token is parsed to binary64 first. Its exact dyadic
value is the u used by the exact comparison. Thus
`0.49999999999999999` rounds to 0.5 and returns 1 for H, whereas the
round-trip spelling `0.49999999999999994` denotes the predecessor of 0.5
and returns 0. A decimal-looking near tie is not necessarily a different
binary64 draw. Both signs of zero denote the endpoint 0. Nonfinite values,
negative values other than negative zero, and 1 or greater reject. Repeated
`--draws` options reject; a comma-separated list is one option.

When the queue is exhausted the current reference uses xoshiro256** state
`[1,2,3,4]`, taking `(next_u64 >> 11)/2^53`. Supplied draws do not advance
that generator. Surplus valid draws are unused, without a leftover-list
diagnostic. These are reproduced/source-derived **CLI** conventions, not
new universal ISA PRNG/seed requirements. An exhausted list still gets a
Born draw under 0.9; do not mandate an exhaustion fault or a zero fallback.
`--entropy` belongs to the effect device and does not seed measurements.
`--seed` is accepted by `step-check` for §12.1 sampling, not by `run-cc`
or `run-program`. Do not equate that sampler with measurement randomness.
A future execution API may expose a seed; replay portability requires it
to state generator, state and number conversion, or supply an explicit
common draw stream. K17 fixes mapping/consumption, not a cross-executor
unsupplied-random-bit identity. No new seeded CLI is proposed.

 This fixes the reference CLI convention; unsupplied draws follow the

+
+For ordered outcomes s_j with Born weights w_j, let T = sum_j w_j > 0
+and C_j = sum_(i<=j) w_i, with C_-1 = 0. Outcome s_j is selected exactly
+when C_(j-1) <= draw*T < C_j; an outcome of weight zero is never selected.
+These comparisons use the Born weights, not rounded display probabilities
+or a tie tolerance. One joint n-wire measurement consumes one draw,

changed without a version bump.

## Conformance vectors and consumer impact

All draws in this table have exact binary64 representations except the
explicit parsing vector. Decimal spellings use round-trip values.

| State / measurements | Draw(s) | Expected wire-first bits; integer(s) |
|---|---|---|
| H\|0> | 0 | `0`; 0 |
| H\|0> | 0.49999999999999994 | `0`; 0 |
| H\|0> | 0.5 | `1`; 1 |
| H\|0> | 0.5000000000000001 | `1`; 1 |
| H\|0> | 0.9999999999999999 | `1`; 1 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.24999999999999997 / 0.25 / 0.25000000000000006 | `00`/`01`/`01`; 0/2/2 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.49999999999999994 / 0.5 / 0.5000000000000001 | `01`/`10`/`10`; 2/1/1 |
| H⊗H\|00> | 0.7499999999999999 / 0.75 / 0.7500000000000001 | `10`/`11`/`11`; 1/3/3 |
| Two fresh \|++> registers, joint measures | 0.25,0.75 | `01`,`11`; 2,3 (published K17 carrier) |
| Fresh deterministic 0, then fresh H\|0> | 0.75,0.25 | `0`,`0`; 0,0 |
| Independent \|++>, measured one wire at a time | 0.25,0.5 | `0`,`1`; 0,1 (conditional normalization) |
| Bell pair, joint | 0.5 | `11`; 3 (unsupported outcomes skipped) |
| Bell pair, separate wires | 0.5,0 | `1`,`1`; 1,1 |
| H\|0>, decimal parsing witness | token 0.49999999999999999 | `1`; 1 after binary64 conversion |

Further acceptance vectors: a repeated call/loop with asymmetric draws;
a branch that skips a measurement; a width/view precondition failure
followed by retry through an API that permits retry, verifying no queue or
PRNG advancement; 128-bit/high-wire values and run-time-width 0.9 measures;
and reversal rejection after any successful source measure. These are
**proposed**, not executions of 0.9 or fault-injection coverage here.

For bennett-vm use strict `CDF > u*T`, wire-first outcome ordering and
conditional weights; retain the stated VM fix if it meets those rules.
Floating roundoff with an epsilon tie test is not this rule. For the
conformance harness serialize explicit common draws, compare bits and
little-endian values, and test FIFO separately from threshold strictness.
Do not mask the H disagreement by dropping exact ties or use two executors'
seed defaults as a portable oracle. 0.8 draws are an explicitly documented
reference/harness profile; 0.9 draws are normative K17. Existing broad
harness claims require their owner's rerun; these finite vectors do not
establish all 32 cases.

register tests filtered); probe passed **28 draw vectors**, the published
K17 carrier and **7 CLI rejection vectors**. Fixtures are generated and
hash-checked only under the job directory. The probe below is exactly the
script run; save it as the named scratch file after making the job's tmp
directory. It checks actual reference CLI results, not a second copy of
the sampler. Its use of the reference hash command to prepare executable
fixtures is not an independent identity proof.

```python
from pathlib import Path
import json, math, os, subprocess
ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-isa-textdefects'
BIN = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
def cli(*args, good=True):
    p = subprocess.run([str(BIN), *map(str, args)], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=15)
    if good:
        assert p.returncode == 0, (args, p.stderr)
        return json.loads(p.stdout)
    assert p.returncode == 1 and not p.stdout and p.stderr, (args, p)
    return p.stderr

def fixture(name, actions, cc, results):
    symbols, bodies = {}, []
    for i, (n, widths, body) in enumerate(actions):
        sig = dict(abi='0.8',args=[],bounds=[],**{'in':[]},kind='action',mem=[],mut=widths,tier='q')
        params = ', '.join(f'q{j}:wire[{w}] mut' for j,w in enumerate(widths))
        symbols[str(i)] = dict(interface_hash='0'*64,kind='action',name=n,signature=sig)
        bodies.append(f'action @{n} ({params}) () () () ()\n^entry():\n{body}\n')
    sig = dict(abi='0.8',kind='cc',params=[],results=[f'i{w}' for w in results],tier='cc')
    symbols[str(len(symbols))] = dict(interface_hash='0'*64,kind='cc',name='main',signature=sig)
    outs = ', '.join(f'r{i}:i{w}' for i,w in enumerate(results))
    bodies.append(f'fn cc @main () ({outs}) () () ()\n^entry():\n{cc}\nret '+ ' '.join(f'r{i}' for i in range(len(results)))+'\n')
    def text():
        return ('bennett-object isa=0.8 object=0.8 launch=0.8 sched=0.8 cert=0.8 coverage=0.8 target=0.8 ptr_width=32\n\nsection symbols\n'+json.dumps(symbols,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':'))+'\n\nsection functions\n'+''.join(bodies))
    path = JOB / (name+'.bisa')
    path.write_text(text())
    hashes = cli('hash', path)['symbols']
    for sym in symbols.values():
        sym['interface_hash'] = hashes[sym['name']]['interface_hash']
    path.write_text(text())
    assert cli('check', path)['status'] == 'ok'
    return path
h1 = [('prep',[1],'h q0[0]')]
one = fixture('one-h', h1, 'q = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[1])
two = fixture('two-h', [('prep',[2],'h q0[0]\nh q0[1]')], 'q = qreg.new any 2\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[2])
bell = fixture('bell', [('prep',[2],'h q0[0]\ncx q0[0] q0[1]')], 'q = qreg.new any 2\naction_call @prep (q) () () ()\nr0 = measure q',[2])
det = fixture('deterministic', [], 'q = qreg.new any 1\nr0 = measure q',[1])
fifo = fixture('det-then-h', h1, 'a = qreg.new any 1\nr0 = measure a\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (b) () () ()\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
conditional = fixture('conditional', [('prep',[1,1],'h q0[0]\nh q1[0]')], 'a = qreg.new any 1\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (a, b) () () ()\nr0 = measure a\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
entangled = fixture('entangled', [('prep',[1,1],'h q0[0]\ncx q0[0] q1[0]')], 'a = qreg.new any 1\nb = qreg.new any 1\naction_call @prep (a, b) () () ()\nr0 = measure a\nr1 = measure b',[1,1])
checks = []
def check(path, draws, bits, values, command='run-cc'):
    actual=cli(command,path,'main','--draws',draws)
    m=actual['measurements']
    assert [x['bits'] for x in m] == bits, (path.name,draws,m,bits)
    assert [int(x['value']) for x in m] == values
    checks.append(dict(fixture=path.name,draws=draws,bits=bits,values=values,command=command))
    return actual
for u,want in [(0.,0),(math.nextafter(.5,0),0),(.5,1),(math.nextafter(.5,1),1),(math.nextafter(1.,0),1)]:
    check(one,repr(u),[str(want)],[want])
for j,u in enumerate([0.,.25,.5,.75]):
    for d,k in [(u,j),(math.nextafter(u,1),j)]+([] if j==0 else [(math.nextafter(u,0),j-1)]):
        bits=['00','01','10','11'][k]
        check(two,repr(d),[bits],[[0,2,1,3][k]])
check(two,repr(math.nextafter(1.,0)),['11'],[3])
check(bell,'0.5',['11'],[3])
check(det,'0.9999999999999999',['0'],[0])
check(fifo,'0.75,0.25',['0','0'],[0,0])
check(conditional,'0.25,0.5',['0','1'],[0,1])
check(entangled,'0.5,0',['1','1'],[1,1])
check(one,'0.49999999999999999',['1'],[1]) # binary64 parsing rounds to 1/2
check(one,'-0',['0'],[0])
check(one,'0.5,0.1',['1'],[1]) # surplus draws do not reject
check(one,'0.5',['1'],[1],command='run-program')
# Default PRNG starts at [1,2,3,4]; these first draws are below 1/2.
check(one,'',['0'],[0])
check(fifo,'0.75',['0','0'],[0,0])
# Published K17's executable carrier is a 0.8 witness, not 0.9 admission.
carrier=ROOT/'conformance/cases/A/A-measure-draw-order-09/two-measures.bisa'
m=cli('run-program',carrier,'twice','--draws','0.25,0.75')['measurements']
assert [(x['bits'],int(x['value'])) for x in m] == [('01',2),('11',3)]
for invalid in ['-0.1','1','NaN','inf','0.5,NaN']:
    error=cli('run-cc',one,'main','--draws',invalid,good=False)
    assert '[0,1)' in error, error
assert 'seed' in cli('run-cc',one,'main','--seed','1,2,3,4',good=False)
assert 'twice' in cli('run-cc',one,'main','--draws','0.5','--draws','0.5',good=False)
(JOB/'draw-results.json').write_text(json.dumps(checks,indent=2)+'\n')
print(f'PASS: {len(checks)} draw vectors; published K17 carrier; 7 CLI rejection vectors')
```

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

# Bennett Virtual ISA — Specification, version 0.9

**Date:** 2026-10-31. **Status:** version 0.9, **accepted by all three council seats on 2026-10-31** (sol r3, codex r2/r4, muse r5; K64/K65 ratified). ISA 0.9 is the version new consumers (bennett-vm M5 onward) implement. ISA 0.8 remains published as `spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` and remains the version the dialect 0.2.x gate and the reference interpreter target until the dialect 0.3 track moves.
Snapshot `59eee9ad0d96` was accepted by the sol and codex seats (council run
`isa-09-2026-10-31`: codex round 2, sol round 3, both core accept, 0 blocking; muse seat held).

§0 lists every change from 0.8.

names items left open; every deferred decision that an implementer must

**Versioning.** ISA 0.9, object 0.9, launch 0.9, sched 0.9, cert 0.9,
coverage 0.9, target 0.9. The **full version string** (`0.9`) is part of
every semantic envelope (§9.2): drafts are not compatible with each
other, so every hash of every 0.8 object differs under 0.9 (§0, §16.3).

---

## 0. Changes from 0.8

Every change from the accepted draft 0.8 has one entry below; nothing
changes without an entry. **Kind**: *E* = editorial (wording, or a
reading pinned that a conformant 0.8 implementation could already take,
with local semantics unchanged); *S* = semantic (some outcome or
well-formedness verdict differs from some reading of 0.8, or a
capability is added). **Hash**: every entry rides on the version bump V,
which changes envelope part (1) and every signature's `abi`, hence the
hash of **every** symbol (§9.2): the four 0.8 example hashes all change
and §16.3 republishes their objects (the 0.8 vectors remain valid 0.8
vectors); "+bytes" marks an entry that also changes the canonical bytes
of functions that use the affected form; "new" marks new symbols.
Sources: *EN* = an item of the evidence note
`docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md`; *bd* = a bead of this
repository; *SQ n* = entry n of `reference-interpreter/SPEC-QUESTIONS.md`
(its reading is adopted unless the entry says otherwise); *RC* = the
reference-interpreter council round 1
(`docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/round-1/report.md` in `bennett`),
member and finding; *r8* = a council round-8 suggestion (bead
`b-isa-spec-zsw.N`); *DL* = the dialect 0.2 outline's §25 list.

**No narrowing.** No entry rejects, faults, declares inert or removes a
capability that 0.8 permits or the north star promises. The entries
that add or sharpen a well-formedness check (E11's static view rule,
E16.9, E17.2, E17.3, E17.5's unmatched uncalls, E17.8, E17.9, E17.10,
E17.12, E17.14, the block terminators of K5) concern spellings that
always have a legal equivalent or uses that 0.8 did not define soundly
(E17.14: a location value as a classical argument would be superposed);
the run-time checks (E11, K1's width checks, K3's token validity, K4's
inverse-pair check) concern operations that 0.8 did not define or
already required (0.8's `qinplace` already demanded its certificates;
K4 admits library certificates as well as exhaustive claims, as the
north star §3.1 requires).

**Decided Q14 (applied 2026-10-29, confirmed 2026-10-30; b-isa-spec-7u6).** Lprocs take explicit
token and classical parameters (§3.7, §4.2, §4.7, §16.2), forwarded by
`lift.call`; mapped-region parameters preserve the complete lifted-call
interface. Fixed binding instances (§3.6–§3.7) keep those arguments
classical under coherent calls, without an extra budget charge (§5.4).
The interface and its hash include the new groups (§9.1–§9.2); §15 and
§16.3 example 10 supply conformance obligations and a witness. Source:
the maintainer's acceptance recorded as "the Q14 edit (lproc
token/classical params)" in the "interpreter milestone 1" session,
`worklog/001_2026-10-28_bootstrap.md` line 26; the orchestrator's
2026-10-29 decision selects explicit parameters. The maintainer's
2026-10-30 ratification confirms this reading: lproc header groups are
`(params)(result)(tokens)(classical)(mem)` and `lift.call` forwards tokens,
classical arguments and mapped regions.

**Decided confirmations (2026-10-30).** The maintainer ratified EPS-DECADE
(K9), complete-sector BLOCK-IMAGE (K10), the reference CLI draw ordering
(K17), ISA-7 `fault C` and its fault-code table (K5), and Q1–Q13 as
already applied. These decisions are recorded in the maintainer memo,
Ratification section, 2026-10-30. Q12 keeps the §16.3 draft example
hashes computed by the 0.9 precheck; independently checked target/frame witness hashes
are published in §8.3 and §9.4.

### 0.3 Group 3: optional efficiency semantics (deferred)

| # | § | Change | Closes | Kind | Hash |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | §17 item 17, §3.6 | Identity-idle step (old index encoded as k ⊕ HALT, idle frames zero), deferred with the printed step as default and its normative hook | EN I5; bd roy | E | – |
| P2 | §17 item 18, §5.7, §9.4 | Checkpointed and pebbled oracle realisations, deferred with full history as default and the expansion-library hook | EN I1; bd 9c3 | E | – |

**Considered and deferred** (efficiency in the evidence note; no 0.9
text; the capability is kept by the stated construction, so no rung and
no north-star promise depends on the deferred primitive):

| Item | Construction that keeps the capability |
|---|---|
| ISA-2 (R-tier budget) | an R step counter threaded through the code; exhaustion is now `fault 1` (K5) |
| ISA-5 (two simultaneous slices) | one action on the parent register (e.g. `cx q[0] q[7]`); run-time indices by dispatch or by run-time slice `lo` (E12) |
| ISA-6 (non-consuming measurement) | CNOT copy of the wires into a fresh register, then `measure` of the copy; the original keeps its id |
| ISA-9 (borrowing driven from CC) | a clean allocation, or a Q region with its own borrow frame |
| ISA-11 (host memory at native addresses) | promotion of the pointer-sharing callers to CC (`alloc`, `hostcall` for genuine host effects) |
| G-12 (run-time snapshot at native addresses) | replay every allocation in bump order (dead and zero-size ones included) and copy the bytes |
| G-13 (interface base chosen at call time) | static arenas and explicit logical-to-physical address translation rows |
| G-14 (step machine inside actions) | bounded, padded structured expansion with immutable selectors |
| G-15 (payloads wider than 128 bits) | realised directly: `tgt`, `reg[N]` and `result_val` have no width bound (E17.1), and lifted suffixes name location widths (E17.15) |
| G-16 (fibers, interrupts) | explicit continuation records in memory and polling at defined points |
| I2 (bounded-loop recovery) | §5.7 structure recovery with immutable selectors or retained history |
| I3 (budget provenance) | host tuple or bounded wrapper; dialect documentation |
| I6 (step-granular oracle interface) | whole-oracle budget ladders of region calls |
| SQLite E1–E5 | logical stack and frames as specified; compare tree plus static `lift.call`; a copy lproc; classical-data specialisation under §5.7 |
| measuring registers wider than 128 bits (bd 04w) | copy 128-bit slices to fresh registers, measure the copies, reset the source wire by wire with run-time slices |

## 1. Scope and conformance

## 4. Instruction set

### 4.1 Classical control (CC, R)

| Instruction | Form | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| `const` | `r = const.iN c` | |
| `add sub mul and or xor shl lshr ashr` | `r = op a b` | wrap; shift ≥ N → 0 |
| `udiv urem sdiv srem` | `r = op a b` | divisor 0 or signed MIN/−1: CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW`, §4.9) |
| `icmp` | `r = icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle} a b` | |
| `select` | `r = select p a b` | |
| `zext sext trunc` | `r = op.iM a` | |
| `ptrtoint` / `inttoptr` | `r = ptrtoint.iM p` / `p = inttoptr a` | zero-extend/truncate to M / to ptr_width |
| `load` / `store` | `r = load.iN addr` / `store.iN addr v` | N/8 bytes little-endian, unaligned; outside every live allocation: CC trap / R fault record. The suffix is always `.iN` with N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}: there is no pointer-typed load or store (a pointer is stored as `ptrtoint.iM` with M = ptr_width and reloaded with `inttoptr`) |
| `alloc` / `free` | `p = alloc n` / `free p` | §7.1 |
| `br` | `br ^b(args)` / `br p ^b1(args) ^b2(args)` | parallel block-parameter assignment |
| `call` / `ret` | `results = call @f args` / `ret vals` | arguments and values are bare operands (`r = call @f a b`, `(r1, r2) = call @f a`, `call @f`, `ret v`, `ret`); results bind in order; a parenthesised argument list is ill-formed for `call`; callee tiers per §3.1 |
| `uncall` | `uncall @f (args) (results)` | **segment uncall**, legal exactly under §7.4: the most recent completed call segment of the history is a call of `@f` whose recorded boundary argument and result values equal the current operand values, every frame after it is pure, and it contains, at any nesting depth, no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified; otherwise CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` with no state change beyond the fault record. The segment is reversed by the cancellation-aware history traversal of §7.4 (LIFO, nested segments included; frames already reversed and `uncall` markers skipped) and its frames are marked reversed; the `uncall` itself pushes a frame naming the segment |
| `trap` | `trap` | CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_TRAP`, §4.9) |
| `fault` | `fault C` | CC and R; literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64; one terminal record with code C for C ≠ 0, else 4 (§5.4); run ends `FAILED`, no successor executes, prefix history retained for reversal (§7.4, §10, §14.1). One classical instruction (§8.2); in an oracle body or lproc, the one-budget-step §4.2 row instead |
| `qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat qreg.width qreg.id qreg.from_id` | §3.3 | CC and R (§3.3) |
| `measure` | `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | CC only; Born rule (§14.2); consumes q; `.iN` for a `qreg[*]` (§3.3) |
| `budget.mint` | `tk = budget.mint @o s h st d` | CC and R; a token for the tuple (s, h, st, d) validated for oracle `@o` (§5.4) |
| `effect` | `effect dev.op args` | R only (§7.3); results are written to memory operands |
| `hostcall` | `rlen = hostcall service ptr len` | CC only (§10) |

**Measurement draws.** Supplied draws are consumed FIFO, one per
executed measurement in dynamic execution order (including measurements
of deterministic states); a failing precondition consumes no draw.
Each draw is in [0,1). Enumerate outcomes lexicographically by their
printed bits string, wire 0 first, and select the first outcome whose
cumulative Born weight is **strictly greater** than the draw times the
total weight. Thus an exact CDF tie selects the next nonzero-weight
outcome. Returned integers remain little-endian (wire 0 is bit 0).
This fixes the reference CLI convention; unsupplied draws follow the
Born distribution (§14.2).

**DRAW-ORDER witness**

```json
{"draws":["0.25","0.75"],"integer_order_values":[1,3],"outcomes_bits":["01","11"],"outcomes_values":[2,3],"reversed_draw_values":[3,2]}
```

Prepare two fresh two-wire registers with H on both wires and measure
them in order. Each has weights 1/4 on `00`, `01`, `10`, `11` in that
order. The first draw selects `01` (integer 2) and the second `11`
(integer 3). Reversed draw consumption would give integers 3,2;
integer-ordered outcomes would give 1,3. Both draws test exact CDF ties.

### 4.2 Lifted rows (oracle bodies and lprocs)

Columns: **Class**, **Reads**, **Writes / slots** (A: displaced values in
slot order; D: the permutation), **Flag / fault** (C = code), **Succ**.
Every row is one budget step; `k` is its own index.

| Row | Form | Class | Reads | Writes / slots | Flag / fault | Succ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lift.const` | `w = lift.const.iN c` | A | – | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.binop` | `w = lift.{add,…,ashr} a b` | A | a, b | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.div` | `w = lift.{udiv,urem,sdiv,srem} a b` | A | a, b | w (under ¬flag) | flag = (b=0) ∨ (signed ∧ a=MIN ∧ b=−1); C = `FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.icmp` | `w = lift.icmp.{pred} a b` | A | a, b | w:reg[1] | – | k+1 |
| `lift.select` | `w = lift.select p a b` | A | p, a, b | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.cast` | `w = lift.{zext,sext,trunc}.iM a` | A | a | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.mov` | `w = lift.mov a` | A | a | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.parmov` | `(w_1..w_m) = lift.parmov (a_1..a_m)` | A | a_i | w_1..w_m (m slots) | – | k+1 |
| `lift.label` | `w = lift.label ^b` | A | – | w | – | k+1 |
| `lift.addr` | `w = lift.addr @g` | A | – | w = layout base of global or mapped region g | – | k+1 |
| `lift.boundscheck` | `lift.boundscheck p n` | D | p, alloctab | – | flag = ¬inrange(p, n); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.load` | `w = lift.load.iN a` | A | the N/8 cells at a.. (§7.2) | w (under ¬flag) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.store` | `lift.store.iN a v` | A | v | the N/8 cells at a.. (under ¬flag; one slot of N bits) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.alloc` | `w = lift.alloc n` | A | n, heap_used, atop | under ¬flag, slots in this order: w ← heap_base + heap_used; alloctab[atop] ← (heap_base + heap_used, n, 1, 0); heap_used ← heap_used + max(n,1); atop ← atop + 1 (4 slots, all computed from the old state, then exchanged in this order, `alloctab[atop]` at the old `atop`). **Inverse exchanges**: atop, heap_used, alloctab[atop] (at the restored `atop`), w; then clear the four slots | flag = (heap_used + max(n,1) > heap_limit) ∨ (atop ≥ 2^A_tab); C = `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.free` | `lift.free p` | D | p, alloctab | match_i = (i < atop) ∧ (base_i = p) ∧ ¬pinned_i into scratch (at most one i matches: unpinned entries are heap entries, whose bases strictly increase); under ¬flag: live_i ^= match_i for all i; scratch uncomputed from the unchanged atop, bases and pinned bits | flag = ¬∃ i: match_i ∧ live_i; C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.faultif` | `lift.faultif p code` | D | p | – | flag = p; C = code, written as its effective cause C′ (§5.4) | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT |
| `lift.call` | `w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)` (short form: §3.7) | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: m capture slots `disp_1..disp_m`, then assignment slots for fp, sp, depth) | flag per §3.7; C = `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` | ¬flag: entry of @f; flag: HALT |
| `lift.ret` | `lift.ret v` | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: the delivery slot `disp_1`, then assignment slots for fp, depth) | flag = (depth = 0); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT |
| `lift.retval` | `lift.retval v` | D | v | result_val ^= v | – | HALT |
| `lift.oracle_call` | `lift.oracle_call @o tk (ins..) tgt fr (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, tgt, fr, mem | tgt ^= payload; fr ^= code (callee contract) | – | k+1 |
| `lift.oracle_uncall` | same form | D | as above | the matched inverse (§5.5): the identical permutation | – | k+1 |
| `lift.perm_call` | `lift.perm_call @a (mut..) (ins..) (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, mut, mem | mut ← P(mut, ins, mem); inverse = `adj` of @a | – | k+1 |
| `qjump` | `qjump ^b` | D | – | – | – | ^b |
| `qbranch` | `qbranch p ^b1 ^b2` | D | p | – | – | p: ^b1; ¬p: ^b2 |
| `qjumpr` | `qjumpr a` | D | a | succ ← a | flag = (a < first_f) ∨ (a ≥ first_f + n_f), where `[first_f, first_f + n_f)` are the row indices of the containing function instance (contiguous, §3.6); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT |
| `fault` | `fault C` | D | – | code ^= C′; faulted ^= 1 (C′ the effective cause of C, §5.4) | – | HALT |

**`inrange(p, n)`** = ∃ i < atop: live_i ∧ base_i ≤ p ∧ p + n ≤ base_i +
size_i, every comparison computed at width max(ptr_width, width(n)) + 1
(no wrap; §3.3); `lift.load`/`lift.store` carry this check
**inherently** on their own address and length, so the reference
function is total for every well-formed body; `lift.boundscheck` is an
explicit assertion row the dialect emits before each access (after it
the access's own flag is never set, so dialect-produced bodies' step
counts are unchanged). Rules: `lift.retval` is ill-formed in an lproc;
`lift.ret` is ill-formed outside lprocs; `tk` is a token-typed classical
argument of the calling oracle or a token parameter of the calling
lproc. `lift.oracle_call`/`uncall`/`perm_call` carry the

## 13. Coverage (coverage 0.9)

`{ "entries": [ { "point": <program-point id>, "source": "<dialect-spec stable id>", "classification": "lowered" | "trapped" | "shimmed", "reason", "fault_code"?, "shim_hash"?, "count" } ] }`.
`fault_code` is present iff `classification` is `trapped`, `shim_hash`
iff it is `shimmed`.

## 14. Formal model

### 14.1 Reference interpreter *(normative)*

Executes an oracle's reference body per basis configuration: locations
and cells are bit-vectors of their declared widths (no upper bound,
§3.3), pseudo-locations read-only, predication is
`if`, each §4.2 row is its classical function (including the inherent
`inrange` check of loads and stores, the activation records of §3.7, the
`alloctab` and stack arrays), `fault` and a triggered `lift.faultif`
record their effective cause (§5.4) and halt, `lift.
retval` halts with success and `f(x) = result_val`, the step counter
counts executed rows (on budget exhaustion exactly B rows have
executed), counters are unbounded integers. It **defines** F_B
and the budget unit and rejects any body that executes a non-permutation
gate.

For CC/R, `fault C` has the terminal-record semantics of §4.9 and the
logical history of §7.4: it preserves the complete executed prefix for
eligible reversal, including activations that have not returned. This
is T4's history traversal, not the Q machine's B inverse steps. In an
oracle body or lproc the same spelling remains the §4.2 fault row;
§3.6 and §5.2 supply its step-machine inverse and T2 prefix reversal.

### 14.2 Quantum state semantics

The state of a Q region is a vector in ⊗ over live wires in allocation
order (bundles little-endian). `qalloc` tensors |0⟩; `qfree` removes
clean factors; `qborrow` introduces a factor in unknown state possibly
entangled with a reference system R, and the region's operation must be
U ⊗ I on it (§5.6); gates act by §4.4 under the active predicate;
`measure` (CC) is the projective computational-basis measurement with
Born probabilities, consuming the wires.

### 14.3 Theorems

- **T1 (totality).** For every well-formed oracle, budget and x ∈ D the
  reference interpreter halts within B + 1 rows and F_B(x) is unique.
- **T2 (prefix reversal).** For every executor whose realisation is a
  composition of ε = 0 permutation gates implementing §5.2 steps 1–4
  with the inverse step of §3.6, on every x ∈ D every non-interface wire
  is clean after step 4 and borrowed bundles hold their initial values.
- **T3 (permutation theorem).** If the realised unitary V of a circuit on
  span(D) is a permutation matrix and the final basis tuple equals P(x)
  for every x ∈ D, then V|span(D) = P.
- **T4 (round trip).** For every finite CC/R trace with retained run
  history and tapes that is eligible for reversal under §10 — a
  `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` run containing no `measure` frame and no
  `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified — the history
  traversal of §7.4 restores the active environment, memory, allocation
  table, device-model state, the ledger entries of every register the
  run created or touched, token validity and active history (§7.4) to
  their entry values; audit records may remain; observed external
  events are not retracted. If, in addition, every region call is undone
  by the exact inverse of its realised forward operation (ε = 0
  realisations, or a realisation that is applied as its own adjoint),
  the quantum state of every register the run touched is restored
  exactly; otherwise to within the sum of the realisations' declared
  bounds (§12.3).
- **T5.** As §6.3.
- **T6 (executor agreement).** Two executors conforming to §3–§7 on one
  target with the same schedule produce on every x ∈ D the same final
  tuple for every oracle (exact) and, for every action with `operator_
  norm` inclusive bounds e₁, e₂, unitaries within e₁ + e₂ in operator
  norm; for channels, diamond bounds d₁ + d₂.

### 14.4 Mechanisation *(deferred)*

## 15. Conformance suites

The K82 named-interface vector is published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k82-09/`: the catalog's interfaces and invoke
edges, expanded leaf digests for those edges and accept/reject binding cases.

The K81 joint-operation / first-release vector is published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k81-09/`: both release orders, indivisible
inverse ownership and a duplicate-maximal cleanup selector tie. Its source
coordinates and ownership are explicitly finite-source-adapter data, not
a production 0.9 partition-builder result.

Portable K78–K80 vectors and reproduction instructions are published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k78-k80-09/`, beside the controlled-action fixture.
The vectors record source admission, complete output tuples and expanded source coordinates; adapter-assigned coordinates are identified separately from production-builder output.

| Suite | Class | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| A | Executor | classical ISA; run history and LIFO reversal with loops, recursion, effects, segment `uncall` (impure suffix traps; nested `uncall` inside an uncalled segment; `uncall` over a `measure` traps; a segment that begins at the run's first instruction, uncalled, then whole-run reversal), `hostcall`, `qreg.*`, fault records (T4); CC `uncall` of a CC segment (§3.1); a use reached before any definition traps 4 whatever the block text order; `qreg[*]` (examples 4, 5): run-time widths 0, 2, 3, an id stored and reloaded, `qreg.from_id` of a stale, consumed or viewed id or of a register owned by another open run traps 4, a width-3 register bound to `wire[2]` or `qreg[2]` traps 4 with no effect, aliases consumed together, ids restored and never reused by reversal, run-time slice `lo` and `len`; `qreg.*` in R with whole-run reversal and segment `uncall` (a hardware-unverified free makes the segment non-uncallable); `budget.mint` valid and invalid and its reversal (example 6); the §4.9 causes — `udiv` by 0 → 5, i8 −128 `sdiv` −1 → 6, `load` at address 0 → 7, `alloc` beyond the heap → 2, `trap` → 4, `fault 1` → 1, `fault 0` → 4 — each with the pre-fault state unchanged and exactly one record; terminal R faults and FAILED-run reversal (example 8), also with `fault 2`, `fault 3` and `fault 18446744073709551615`: exact record code, no caller continuation or subsequent source effect, earlier memory/device/tape history intact until reversal |
| B | Executor | oracle totality and prefix reversal: every fault code; B = 0; boundary halts; `lift.call`/`lift.ret` recursion with argument capture; one forward `lift.call` and one forward `lift.ret` step of a recursive call each followed by its inverse (§3.7); stack exhaustion (`sp + R_f > stack_limit`); matched `lift.oracle_uncall`, including two calls followed by two uncalls; interface regions restored; pre-entries; `lift.free` on pinned entries, and of a heap entry whose base equals a size-0 global's (the global stays live); inherent load/store checks; row indices of multi-row blocks and their `lift.label` values; `qjumpr` to an in-range interior row enters it and to an out-of-range index faults 4; `fault 0` and a triggered `lift.faultif p 0` commit `FAULT_UB_TRAP`; `fault C` as the B-th executed row reports C′, whereas a cut just before that row reports 1; subsequent HALT steps and inverse steps preserve the §5.2 outcome and clean every frame; counters at the §3.7 minimum widths and interface widths above 128 bits |
| C | Executor, Producer | T3 over full D at exhaustive widths for oracles, permutation actions, `qinplace` pairs, borrow frames; T6 agreement among VM, specialiser, transformed forms and the reference interpreter; the §16.3 examples as normative test vectors, including the stack-capacity boundary of example 2 and example 3 over its 8 basis and 16 controlled configurations with an entangled reference on the borrowed wire; `qinplace` with `ins` and `args` (example 7) over all x, y, a and both control values, scratch clean, and its adjoint (covered by the same entry); example 10's lproc token/classical/region forwarding over every x, a and byte d, arbitrary target/fault contents, and an external reference, with successful and insufficient nested budgets; coherent calls to two binding instances of one lproc, including recursive forwarding, and matched `lift.oracle_uncall` through parameters; a library (`inverse_pair_library`) claim covering every `args` value; a token argument whose tuple differs from `arg_tokens` traps 4 |

| E | Target author, Executor | JCS parsing; AST evaluation; matching; scheme expansion and correctness; aggregation from event lists; timing model; imports |
| F | Producer | versioning, disjointness incl. control context; operand schema; row classes and frame sizing; index-space flattening; terminators; name resolution; permutation-only oracles; §6 transfer and windows; adjoint table; translation lemma side conditions with the four negative tests; `in`-wire use; acyclic action graphs; grammar; schemas; the §3.1 call-tier table (R → CC rejected); envelope and hashes, acyclic (inc8, zero, sum_db) and cyclic (sum, and a mutual SCC); SCC placement in the index space (mutual f, g whose member hashes order opposite to their names); empty data lines (`NAME SP LF` accepted, two spaces rejected); canonical `INT`/`FLOAT` at every literal site; const literal ranges; the region call graph with lifted calls and `qinplace`; `call` operand spelling; param role suffixes; interface-region names; local regions as call operands; the widened `in`-wire uses of §5.1; LIFO matched uncalls and an unmatched uncall rejected; the static view rule of §3.3 (a view passed through `br`, used twice, or left unconsumed is rejected); lifted-call `args` that are locations rejected; lproc header groups and `token`/`classical` suffixes (a suffix in the wrong group rejected), exact argument counts/types/region roles, missing nonempty lists and location-as-token/classical rejected, forwarding `iN ptr f64 token` without bit conversion, empty-list short form, fixed-binding closure and instance ordering (§3.7), lproc signatures including empty arrays and changed interface/closure hashes; `qreg[*]` typing, the `measure.iN` form and width checks; `budget.mint` rejected in regions; CC/R block terminators; `qinplace` signature compatibility, its short form exactly when the three lists are empty, and its §6.1 transfer (cleaning a value that depends on x after changing an `ins` wire is rejected); CC/R `fault` literal range; linking; frame-descriptor live ranges |

| H | Scheduler | §6 validity; capacity; reproducibility; specificity resolution; verbatim execution; INFEASIBLE/UNSUPPORTED; invocation contexts covering default-only invocations (an action calling one region twice, one call scheduled explicitly: a claim with one witness rejects, two passing witnesses accept); a device-minted tuple binds the schedule (a schedule bound to B = 3 is not applied to an invocation minted with B = 2, which runs under the defaults) |

**Suite H / §11.1 K77 adapter vector.** Captured `oracle_invocation`
follows the source call's arguments and body/template capture, irrespective
of unrelated apply-site context; nested calls restore the enclosing context
on exit. K77 conformance includes the same §16.3 `inc8` closure and
positional vector `[x,t,f]` captured with B=2 and B=3, forwarded through
`invoke`/`call` and captured body/template adapters under a conflicting
apply-site context. Nested calls use their own explicit tuples. Assert
frame counts 2 and 3, final `(t,f)=(x+1 mod 256,1)` from zero seeds,
exact inverse restoration, and retained forward `call_index` values during
inverse traversal (0/1 for the two calls within one source-row expansion;
separate source points each start at 0). Reject absent, malformed or
source-inconsistent records before concrete expansion. This vector tests
the existing K77 binding rule; it adds no catalog argument or binding rule.

# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

  "_type": "issue",

fn measurements_json(m: &ClassicalMachine) -> Json {
    Json::Array(
        m.measurements()
            .into_iter()
            .map(|(reg, bits)| {
                // Wide measurements keep their exact bit-vector value.
                let mut value = BitVector::zero(bits.len()).expect("measured width");
                for (i, b) in bits.iter().enumerate() {
                    value.set(i, *b).expect("measured bit");
                }
                object([
                    (
                        "bits".into(),
                        string(
                            bits.iter()
                                .map(|b| if *b { '1' } else { '0' })
                                .collect::<String>(),
                        ),
                    ),
                    ("reg".into(), number(reg as u128)),
                    ("value".into(), vector_number(&value)),
                ])
            })
            .collect(),
            "--replies" => {
                if !once.insert(option) {
                    return Err(fail("--replies given twice"));
                }
                options.replies = Some(value.clone());
            }
            "--tape-out" | "--replay-tape" => {
                if !once.insert(option) {
                    return Err(fail(format!("{option} given twice")));
                }
                if option == "--tape-out" {
                    options.tape_out = Some(value.clone());
                } else {
                    options.replay_tape = Some(value.clone());
                }
            }
            "--draws" => {
                if !once.insert(option) {
                    return Err(fail("--draws given twice"));
                }
                if !value.is_empty() {
                    options.draws = value
                        .split(',')
                        .map(|d| d.parse::<f64>().map_err(fail))
                        .collect::<Result<_>>()?;
                }
            }
            "--budget" => {
                budget4_seen |= value.matches(',').count() == 3;
                limits = budget(value, limits)?;
                options.fuel = limits.steps;
                options.token_defaults = limits;
            }
            "--mem" => {
                let (name, hex) = value
                    .split_once('=')
                    .ok_or_else(|| fail("--mem requires name=hex"))?;
                if options.mem.iter().any(|(n, _)| n == name) {
                    return Err(fail(format!("duplicate --mem {name}")));
                }
                options.mem.push((name.into(), parse_hex(hex)?));
            }
            "--steps" => {
                if command != "reverse" {
                    return Err(fail("--steps is a reverse option"));
                }
    Json::Array(
        args.iter()
            .filter_map(|a| match a {
                ClassicalValue::Token(b) => Some(object([
                    ("depth".into(), number(b.depth)),
                    ("heap".into(), number(b.heap)),
                    ("stack".into(), number(b.stack)),
                    ("steps".into(), number(b.steps as u128)),
                ])),
                _ => None,
            })
            .collect(),
    )
}
fn classical_machine<'a>(
    program: &'a Object,
    name: &str,
    options: &CcOptions,
) -> Result<(ClassicalMachine<'a>, Json)> {
    let entry = classical_entry(program, name)?;
    let (args, registers) = classical_args(program, entry, &options.args, options.token_defaults)?;
    let budgets = budgets_json(&args);
    let mut machine = ClassicalMachine::with_quantum_registers_and_globals(
        program,
        name,
        &args,
        &options.entropy,
        registers,
        &options.mem,
    )?;
    for draw in &options.draws {
        machine.push_measurement_draw(*draw)?;
    }
    Ok((machine, budgets))
}
fn run_cc(program: &Object, name: &str, options: CcOptions) -> Result<Json> {
    let mut tape = EffectTapeSession::load(&options)?;
    let mut replies = load_replies(options.replies.as_deref())?;
    let (mut machine, budgets) = classical_machine(program, name, &options)?;
    let mut trace = Vec::new();
    let steps = drive(
        &mut machine,
        &mut replies,
        options.fuel,
        None,
        Some(&mut trace),
        tape.as_mut(),
    )?;
    let f = program
        .functions
        .get(name)
        .ok_or_else(|| earg(format!("unknown CC entry @{name}")))?;
    if f.kind != "cc" {
        return Err(earg(format!(
            "entry @{name} has kind {}; a launch executes a device CC entry",
            f.kind
        )));
    }
    let (args, registers) = classical_args(program, f, &options.args, options.token_defaults)?;
    let budgets = budgets_json(&args);
    let mut machine = ClassicalMachine::with_quantum_registers_and_globals(
        program,
        name,
        &args,
        &options.entropy,
        registers,
        &options.mem,
    )?;
    for draw in &options.draws {
        machine.push_measurement_draw(*draw)?;
    }
    let entry = machine.snapshot();
    let entry_digest = machine.memory_digest()?;
    let mut replies = load_replies(options.replies.as_deref())?;

        &mut machine,
        &mut replies,
        options.fuel,
        None,
        None,
        tape.as_mut(),
    )?;
    check_replies_consumed(&replies)?;
    let outcome = machine.outcome();
    let failed = outcome.fault.is_some();

29. **Measurement draw order (§14.2)** *new (round 5, muse suggestion)*.
    Question: projective Born probabilities do not specify how a supplied draw
    maps to an outcome. Reading: outcomes follow lexicographic order of their
    printed bits string (wire 0 first), independently of the little-endian
    integer value. Select the first outcome whose cumulative exact weight is
    strictly greater than draw × total weight; an exact CDF tie selects the
    next outcome. The distribution is unchanged. *Proposed 0.9:* "Reference
    measurement draws enumerate outcomes lexicographically by the bits string
    (wire 0 first), selecting the first cumulative Born weight exceeding the
    draw times total weight." Pin:
    `registers::muse_r5_measurement_draws_order_bits_lexicographically`.

30. **Translation-frame maximality in structured bodies (§5.6)**
    *new (round 5, muse suggestion)*. Question: does “maximal contiguous
    permutation-only subcircuits” mean maximal in unrolled execution or within
    the static instruction sequence containing the endpoints? Reading: within
    the containing static qloop/qif body sequence, consistent with canonical
    nested-line points and independently checked frame instances. The region
    still satisfies factorisation at every frame/return; this does not waive
    any borrowed touch or lifetime obligation. *Proposed 0.9:* "Maximality of

    sequence." Pin:
    `r1_certificates::muse_r5_translation_frame_maximality_is_within_its_static_sequence`
    has two pre-loop identity touches followed by a loop-body frame at ordinals
null outside the §5.4 table), plus `fault`, that code or null. Tape entries
are `{"args","bytes","dev","op"}`: the §7.3 tape triple plus the bytes the
operation transferred, hex. Measurements are `{"bits","reg","value"}` with
`bits` in wire order (wire 0 first). Draws select the first outcome, in
lexicographic order of the bits string (wire 0 first), whose cumulative Born
weight exceeds draw × total weight; integer values remain little-endian
(SPEC-QUESTIONS 29). Console output is `console_hex` always
and `console` when it is valid UTF-8, else null. `memory_digest` is the
SHA-256 of the complete §7.1 memory (allocation table, heap bump, every
nonzero byte).

or count mismatch): `--args a,b`, `--input a,b`, or repeated `--arg value`,
at most one form (omission means no arguments). An `iN`/`ptr` value is decimal (negatives accepted within

binary64; `token:B[,heap,stack,depth]` is a §5.4-validated budget token
(the four-component `--budget` supplies its defaults, as for `run-oracle`);
`qreg:<bits>` allocates a fresh register and prepares those basis bits
(first character is wire 0). `--entropy hex` is the entropy tape. `--draws
d,...` queues measurement draws in [0, 1). `--replies file` is a JSON array

HOST_REQUEST order: each request must match the next entry's service and
payload, every request must find a reply, and every reply must be consumed,
else the run is a §10 diagnostic and no JSON is printed. `--mem global=hex`
replaces a layout global's image in the entry state, at exactly its size.
