sed -n '1744,1758p' spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md
cat ../bennett/docs/council/isa-09-k82-2026-11-01/VERDICT.md" in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec
 succeeded in 0ms:
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
neither reuses the original computation's edges or scratch identities.
Callees retain their own source points, nested chains and ordinal/call
spaces under these fresh edges. Internal replay does not import the
original rows' source-qloop coordinates. Raw inverse traversal retains
forward paths, leaf ordinals, call occurrences and scratch identities;
step/phase follows §11 and source-adjoint formation remains K62. The carrier's ordinal 0 belongs only to
cleanup-kind matching; it does not consume emitted-leaf ordinal 0.
K64's oracle support partition is unchanged.

**Multirow cleanup witness (K80).** In one action invocation, with no
source qloop, write six separate source instructions:

```
^entry():
d = qalloc any 1
cx x d
cx z d
cx z d
cx x d
qfree d
```

The carrier is `entry:5`, cleanup ordinal 0 in either realisation.
The original allocation and computation stay at `entry:0/1/2`, each at
leaf ordinal 0. Reverse emits `cx z d`, `cx x d`, release d at
`entry:3/4/5`, each at leaf ordinal 0. Recompute replaces those three
rows by this caller trace, all at `entry:5`:

| Leaf ordinal | Operation |
|---|---|
| 0 | allocate fresh u[1] clean, class any |
| 1 | cx x u |
| 2 | cx z u |
| 3 | cx u d (the XOR) |
| 4 | cx z u |
| 5 | cx x u |
| 6 | release u |
| 7 | release d |

Thus a gate selector at `entry:1/generated:0` selects only the original
CX; `entry:5/generated:0` has an allocation and no gate, while
`entry:5/generated:1` selects the fresh computation's first CX.
The carrier has no charge: cleanup gate counts are two for reverse,
five for recompute, before target decomposition. If the two computing
CXs are instead two calls to a linked one-CX perm_action, reverse keeps
the written inverse calls at `entry:3/4`, each with call_index 0.
Recompute has fresh call_index 0/1 for computation and 2/3 for
uncomputation at `entry:5`, in emitted call order; each one-CX callee
starts at its own source point and ordinal 0. The XOR remains caller
leaf ordinal 1, with allocation u at 0 and releases u/d at 2/3.
Reversing that already expanded trace visits call_index 3/2/1/0 and
caller ordinals 3/2/1/0 without renumbering.

**Generated operations and the expansion library.** Every row and
instruction expands into **generated operations** (gates, allocations,
releases). Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6
and §5.2 (dispatch comparators k = 0..n in index order; a row's
sub-operations in listed order; successor XORs; commit; cleanup). Below
that level (the gate decomposition of comparators, adders, multiplexers,
`inrange` scans and their scratch) the expansion is defined by an
**expansion library**: a versioned document, identified in the
schedule's `bindings.expansion` as `{ "name", "version", "hash" }`, that
lists for every row kind and every instruction its generated operations
in order, with their **ordinals**, scratch identities and the subset it
marks removable, and that is extensionally equal to the reference (§5.7,
T6). A consumer that does not implement the bound library rejects the
schedule (`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`); two consumers implementing it
execute and charge the same operation set. `bennett-isa-spec` publishes
the reference library `ref` (§17 item 7). §5.7 transformations under a
## 8. Target descriptions (target 0.9)

### 8.1 Format and expressions

A target is a JSON document in RFC 8785 canonical form; `target_hash` =
SHA-256 of those bytes; numbers are exact rationals from their decimal
text. Cost expressions are a closed JSON AST:

```
  expr := number | {"var": v} | {"op": o, "args": [expr, …]} | {"table": name, "index": "k" | "eps_decade"}
  v    := "k" | "c" | "eps" | "a" | "w" | "i" | "theta"
  o    := "+" (2) | "-" (2) | "*" (2) | "/" (2) | "max" (2) | "min" (2) | "ceil" (1) | "log2inv_eps" (0)
```

`log2inv_eps` = the exact integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 2^j ≥ 1 } for 0 <

# K82: binding the ref@0.9 helper recipes

2026-11-01. Stream opus-isa-helpers, for `b-isa-spec-g2q` and `bennett-sched-enz`. **Proposal only**: `spec/` is unchanged and the council has not seen this.

## Verdict

This is a spec defect. Existing text does not bind these helpers; read literally, it requires consumers to reject the published catalog. The recommended fix is a new rule, K82, that changes no catalog byte and keeps hash `5de4e499...d197`.

## What the text says

- §11.1 K19 (spec lines 2924, 2984-2990): `invoke` binds `args` positionally, consumers MUST reject an unbound name, and "There are no implicit bindings or defaults."
- The catalog: `value.add(N)` reads `a`, `b`, `t` and `bind.value.add` invokes it with only `[N]`. So a strictly lexical consumer rejects it, and the sched test `undeclared_helper_operands_are_reported_with_the_contract_gap` records exactly that.
- K77 to K81 do not help. K77 covers only the oracle token channel and says it is "not an implicit name". K78 to K81 cover entry analysis, empty addresses and cleanup.
- §17 item 7 and K57 only choose the library and its hash.
- Two passages show the intent without binding it: the ISA-REF-LIB witness treats `s`, `t` as the interface of an integer-only `qxor`, and K20 speaks of "recipe interface wires". Neither says how `invoke` supplies those names.

## What the catalog and consumers actually do

| Item | Finding | How established |
|---|---|---|
| Recipes with free names | 54 of 219, exactly those whose formals are all bare names or empty | `generate.py` for the 54; a heuristic sweep found no free name in the other 165 |
| `invoke` sites into the 54 | 20: 17 `bind.value.*`, plus `lift.sext`, `lift.trunc`, `lift.zext`; all bind each name as an identically named wire formal | `generate.py` asserts this |
| The other 34 | Never invoked; reachable only by direct instantiation (historical helpers, `qxor`, `gate.*`, and so on) | `generate.py` |
| Reference `ref_language.rs` | A recipe with all-bare formals imports all caller wires by name (`c.legacy`, `bind_seeded`); no other recipe imports anything | code read and probe |
| VM `src/ref_library.rs` | `bind_formals` starts from `capture.clone()`, so **every** recipe inherits **every** caller binding, typed ones and non-wires included | code read only; VM not run |
| Sched `src/expansion.rs` | Strictly lexical, so it rejects the catalog | code read and its own test |

Consumers therefore disagree today. The reference and VM accept the catalog by two different undocumented rules, and sched rejects it. The VM rule should give the same leaves on this catalog, since the typed recipes have no free names, but it would accept a typed recipe with a free name. That is inferred, not executed.

## Why a declared width is not part of the rule

Exact widths cannot be inferred from what a body reads: `value.trunc` reads `M` bits of an `N`-bit `a`, `value.udiv` reads 3 bits of a 4-bit `f`, and `value.zext` reads `N` bits of an `M`-bit `t`. K82 therefore range-checks reads and leaves equal-width checks to the typed formals of the invoking recipe. All 20 sites have such formals. A direct instantiation of one of the 34 has no such check beyond its explicit name map: a too-wide list is accepted and its extra wires go unread.

## Options

| Option | Catalog hash | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| **K82, named interface (recommended)** | Unchanged | Sound on all 20 sites, closes every other recipe, and matches the leaves the reference already emits. It is an explicit rule, not a guessed fallback. |
| Rewrite 54 recipes with typed formals (the bead's proposal) | Changes | Cleaner language, same leaves, but it re-pins every consumer below and invalidates published schedule and certificate bytes in exchange for no semantic gain. |
| Keep the text, have consumers adopt the reference behaviour | Unchanged | Rejected: it leaves the contract contradicting itself. |

## Hash impact per consumer

K82 needs no re-pin anywhere. The table shows what the rejected rewrite would have to re-pin.

| Consumer | Pins | Change needed under K82 |
|---|---|---|
| ISA spec | §11.1 text (2 places), `spec/ref-0.9.json` | The diff below; regenerate `spec/isa-0.8-to-0.9.diff` |
| Reference | `reference-interpreter/src/ref_library.rs` | `b-isa-spec-b5b` (import exactly the interface) |
| Conformance in this repo | `controlled-action-09` (`schedule.json`, `controlled.bisa`, README, tool, test), `draft_witnesses.py` | None |
| bennett-vm | `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`, `src/ref_library.rs`, `tests/ref09.rs` | Close typed recipes; import only interface wires |
| bennett-sched | `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`, `src/schedule.rs`, `tests/canonical_cost.rs` | Implement K82; replace the test named above; then close `bennett-sched-enz` |
| bennett-targets | `docs/verification.json` | None |
| bennett-estimate | `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`, `src/estimate.py`, `tests/test_estimate.py` | None known; its expansion was not read |
| bennett-conformance | `checker/src/cert.rs`, `checker/tests/core_witnesses.rs`, `clauses/ISA-0.9*` | Regenerate the clause files after the spec edit; K82 adds clauses |

## Evidence

Vector: `conformance/fixtures/pending-k82-09/` (see its README to reproduce).

- `generate.py --check`: OK, with 54 recipes, 20 edges, 86 instances and 16 cases; it also asserts the published envelope hash.
- Probe against the reference expander: 86/86 edge digests match an independent Python expansion that imports only the interface.
- Binding cases: 14/16 pass, and each `reject` case was confirmed to fail for the intended reason. The two failures are the real divergences in `b-isa-spec-b5b`:
  - `callee-local-is-not-captured-by-an-unrelated-caller-name`: `allocation shadows live wire z`
  - `live-declaration-binds-by-its-wire-actual-rule`: `unbound wire q`

Not done: the VM and sched were not run against the vector, the repo gate was not run because no gated file changed, and the sweep over the 165 typed recipes is a heuristic. Edge tuples cover widths 1 to 4.

## Follow-up beads for the orchestrator to file

This stream owns only this repo's `.beads`.

- **bennett-vm**: after K82, `bind_formals` must start each typed recipe from an empty environment and import only interface wires for named-interface recipes. Vector case `typed-recipe-is-closed` is expected to fail today (from reading the code).
- **bennett-sched** (under `bennett-sched-enz`): implement K82 in `invoke`, replace `undeclared_helper_operands_are_reported_with_the_contract_gap`, and pass the vector.

operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`),
classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`,
`to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or
inverse (`inverse`, `body`). Constructor objects have exactly those
keys. Fully instantiated `operations` contain only emitted leaves, each
also carrying its computed `ordinal`; unexpanded `body` constructors
carry no ordinal. Scalars are integer literals, parameter names, or prefix arrays
`[op, operands…]` for `+ - * div mod bit`; div is floor division with a
positive divisor. `bit(x,i)` requires nonnegative integers x and i and
returns floor(x/2^i) mod 2, with no fixed machine-word truncation (K23).
For x=13, bit(x,0..4) is 1,0,1,1,0; literal construction emits X only
|---|---|---|
| Copy, XOR, exchange; native gates | `qxor`: CX s[i] t[i]; `qcopy`: allocate then the same; `quncopy`: the same then release; `qexch`: SWAP a[i] b[i]. Keep each §4.4 gate as a leaf; target schemes expand later. | §4.5: "`cx s[i] t[i]`" and "`swap a[i] b[i]`"; §8.3: "Every template gate MUST itself match a gates entry". |
| Ripple add/sub and comparisons | c[0]=0; p[i]=a[i] XOR b[i]; sum[i]=p[i] XOR c[i]; c[i+1]=(a[i] AND b[i]) XOR (p[i] AND c[i]); compute each bit in this order, XOR sums out, reverse scratch. Subtraction adds bitwise NOT b with c[0]=1. Unsigned a<b is NOT the final subtraction carry; equality is the AND of NOT(a[i] XOR b[i]) in increasing-bit left-associated order. `ule` is OR(ult,eq); signed comparison selects sign(a) if signs differ, else ult. | §17 item 7: "ripple-carry adders and comparators"; §4.1: "wrap"; §3.7: prospective sums use "no wrap" (retain the extra carry for those). |
| Other arithmetic, casts, select | AND/XOR/OR use the Boolean trees above; select emits ((NOT p) AND b[i]) XOR (p AND a[i]). Multiply folds shifted partial products in increasing multiplier-bit order through ripple add modulo 2^N. Shifts scan shift amounts j=0..N−1, equality-selecting the corresponding source bit; ≥N produces zero. Sign-extend replicates the high source bit, zero-extend supplies zero, truncate drops high bits. Division uses restoring unsigned long division, scanning dividend bits from N−1 to 0: append that bit to an N+1-bit remainder, compare with the zero-extended divisor, subtract iff ≥, and emit that predicate as quotient bit. Signed forms conditionally negate operands, divide magnitudes, negate quotient iff signs differ and remainder iff dividend negative; row fault predicates precede these outputs. | §4.2: "w (under ¬flag)" and the div fault predicates; §4.1 wrap/shift rules and signed MIN/−1 fault; §3.3 equal operand widths. |
| Linear inrange and flags | For table entries i=0..2^A_tab−1 compute `(i<atop) AND live_i AND (base_i≤p) AND (p+n≤base_i+size_i)` in that parenthesisation at §4.2's widened width; left-fold OR from false, then uncompute entry scratch. `free` and allocator/call fault trees use their explicit §4.2/§3.7 formula and slot order (including 0.9's E17.13 correction). | §4.2: "inrange(p,n) = ∃ i < atop ..." and "no wrap"; §17 item 7: "linear inrange scans". |
| Address dispatch and multiplexers | Scan every address i in increasing numeric order; compute equality to i, copy/exchange cell bits in increasing order under that bit, uncompute equality before the next i. Lifted memory chooses the §7.2 mapped region in address order and scans its cells in increasing address order; captures use old addresses before exchanged counters. | §4.6: "for each cell i: under a == i"; §17 item 7: "one-hot address dispatch"; §3.7 C1–C5/R1–R5. |
| Row, oracle and structure | Compose these recipes in the printed §3.6 step order, §4.2 slot order, §3.7 activation order, §5.2 allocate/forward/commit/reverse/release order. `qif`, loops, local-region image X gates and `qinplace` use the literal sequences of §4.5–§4.6, incorporating 0.9's explicit parameters. Cleanup `reverse` adjoints that sequence; `recompute` follows §6.4. | §11: "Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6 and §5.2"; §5.2: "run the inverse step for t = B..1, then release (1)". |

The permutation algorithms define exact permutation circuits, preserving
every input required by the row and cleaning scratch; circuit correctness must still be checked
against full-domain T3 witnesses and §5.7, not inferred from costs or
names. Complete catalog: **219 recipes**; envelope SHA-256
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`. [deps: ISA-11.1-P2, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P28, ISA-11.1-P29]
Publication follows acceptance. [deps: ISA-11.1-P1, ISA-11.1-P2] In particular this is
not permission to publish a partial library that omits division, stack,
coherent control, memory or any instruction.

**ISA-REF-LIB witness**

```json
{"isa":"0.9","name":"ref","recipes":[{"kind":"qxor","operations":[{"gate":"cx","ordinal":0,"wires":["s[0]","t[0]"]},{"gate":"cx","ordinal":1,"wires":["s[1]","t[1]"]}],"removable":[],"scratch":[],"source":"0.8 §4.5","width":2}],"version":"0.9"}
```

This is a single instantiated recipe witness, **not** a complete
library document for binding a schedule. Its envelope is the four-line
envelope above with this entire JSON as line four. It has two ordinals,
0 and 1, no scratch and no removable operations. For every two-bit s,t
(16 tuples), the result is `(s, t XOR s)`; reversing visits ordinal 1
then 0 and restores the entry tuple. Its hash identifies only these
witness bytes, not `ref@0.9` as a whole.

Witness envelope SHA-256: `299510fd67cc626fdcbcc10adcd40730f9af294ba596d86fb25fd31be08a5c7f`.

## 12. Certificate (cert 0.9; JCS)
"""Generate the complete draft catalog or historical family checkpoints. Python -I, stdlib only.
No interpreter imports. Does not publish or update the normative hash marker.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import runpy
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
def jcs(v):
    # This catalog uses integer numbers below 2**53; no floats. UTF-16 key order.
    if type(v) is bool: return "true" if v else "false"
    if isinstance(v, dict):
        return '{' + ','.join(jcs(k)+':'+jcs(v[k]) for k in sorted(v, key=lambda k:k.encode('utf-16be'))) + '}'
    if isinstance(v, list):
        return '[' + ','.join(map(jcs,v)) + ']'
    if isinstance(v, str):
        return json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(',', ':'))
    if type(v) is int and abs(v) < 2**53:
        return str(v)
    raise ValueError(f'outside catalog JCS subset: {v!r}')
def plus(a,b): return ['+',a,b]
def minus(a,b): return ['-',a,b]
def times(a,b): return ['*',a,b]
def wire(name, bit=0): return name+'['+jcs(bit)+']'
def gate(g,*w): return {'gate':g,'wires':list(w)}
def alloc(n,w): return {'alloc':n,'width':w,'class':'any','state':'clean'}
def release(n): return {'release':n}
def loop(i,n,b): return {'for':i,'from':0,'to':n,'body':b}
def inv(b): return {'inverse':True,'body':b}
class Circuit:
    def __init__(self): self.body=[]; self.scratch=[]; self.serial=0
    def temp(self,width=1):
        n=f'z{self.serial}'; self.serial+=1
        self.scratch.append(n); self.body.append(alloc(n,width)); return n
    def bit(self): return wire(self.temp())
    def boolean(self,e,out):
        """Postorder tree, fresh scratch per node, no CSE; root XOR out."""
        computes=[]; names=[]
        def visit(e):
            if isinstance(e,str): return e
            op,*args=e
            if op=='or': return visit(('xor',('xor',*args),('and',*args)))
            ws=[visit(a) for a in args]
            n=self.temp(); names.append(n); d=wire(n)
            if op=='not': ops=[gate('x',d),gate('cx',ws[0],d)]
            elif op=='xor': ops=[gate('cx',s,d) for s in ws]
            elif op=='and': ops=[gate('cx',ws[0],d)] if ws[0]==ws[1] else [gate('ccx',*ws,d)]
            else: raise ValueError(op)
            self.body.extend(ops); computes.extend(ops); return d
        root=visit(e); self.body.append(gate('cx',root,out)); self.body.append(inv(computes))
        self.body.extend(release(n) for n in reversed(names))
    def ripple(self,a,b,n,sub=False):
        p=self.temp(n); s=self.temp(n); c=self.temp(plus(n,1))
        body_start=len(self.body)
        if sub: self.body.append(gate('x',wire(c)))
        start=len(self.body)
        aa=a('i'); bb=b('i')
        self.boolean(('xor',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),wire(p,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i')),wire(s,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',('and',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),('and',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i'))),wire(c,plus('i',1)))
        bitbody=self.body[start:]; self.body[start:]=[loop('i',n,bitbody)]
        return s,c,body_start
    def finish(self,outops,start=0):
        compute=self.body[start:]; self.body=self.body[:start]+compute+outops+[inv(compute)]
    def recipe(self,kind,params,source):
        return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':self.body,'scratch':self.scratch,'removable':[], 'source':source}
def recipe(kind,params,body,source,scratch=()):
    return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':body,'scratch':list(scratch),'removable':[],'source':source}
def copy_family():
    out=[]
    for k in ['qxor','qcopy','quncopy','qexch']:
        op=gate('swap',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')) if k=='qexch' else gate('cx',wire('s','i'),wire('t','i'))
        body=[loop('i','N',[op])]
        if k=='qcopy': body.insert(0,alloc('t','N'))
        if k=='quncopy': body += [inv([alloc('t','N')])]
        out.append(recipe(k,['N'],body,'0.8 §4.5: '+('swap a[i] b[i]' if k=='qexch' else 'cx s[i] t[i]')))
    for k,ws in [('x',['a']),('cx',['a','b']),('ccx',['a','b','t']),('swap',['a','b'])]:
        out.append(recipe('gate.'+k,[],[gate(k,*map(wire,ws))],'0.8 §4.4: '+k+' is exact, including global phase.'))
    return out
def arithmetic_family():
    out=[]
    for op in ['add','sub']:
        c=Circuit(); s,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',op=='sub')
        compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [loop('j','N',[gate('cx',wire(s,'j'),wire('t','j'))]),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: add sub wrap; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    for op in ['eq','ne','ult','ule','slt','sle']:
        c=Circuit();carry=None;eq=None
        if op in ['ult','ule','slt','sle']: _,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',True)
        if op in ['eq','ne','ule','sle']:
            eq=c.temp(plus('N',1)); c.body.append(gate('x',wire(eq)))
            start=len(c.body)
            c.boolean(('and',wire(eq,'i'),('not',('xor',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')))),wire(eq,plus('i',1)))
            c.body[start:]=[loop('i','N',c.body[start:])]
        u=('not',wire(carry,'N')) if carry else None; e=wire(eq,'N') if eq else None
        v={'eq':e,'ne':('not',e),'ult':u,'ule':('or',u,e)}.get(op)
        if v is None:
            sa=wire('a',minus('N',1)); sb=wire('b',minus('N',1)); diff=('xor',sa,sb)
            signed=('xor',('and',diff,sa),('and',('not',diff),u))
            v=signed if op=='slt' else ('or',signed,e)
        dest=c.temp(); c.boolean(v,wire(dest)); compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [gate('cx',wire(dest),wire('t')),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.icmp.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle}; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    return out

def build(families):
    recipes=copy_family()
    if families>=2: recipes+=arithmetic_family()
    if families>=3: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_arithmetic.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=4: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_flags.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=5: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_dispatch.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=6: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_bound.py'))['classical'](globals())
    if families>=7: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_bound.py'))['rows'](globals())
    if families>=8: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_memory.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=9: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_structure.py'))['structure'](globals())
    if families>=10:
        recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_structure.py'))['native'](globals())
        recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_memory.py'))['call_flag'](globals())
    if families>=11: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_step.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=12: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_layout.py'))['family'](globals())
    if families>=13: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_allocations.py'))['family'](globals())

    pub fn prepare(&mut self, id: u64, bits: &[bool]) -> Result<()> {

    }

    fn constant(c: Coefficient) -> Self {

    fn monomial(e: BigInt, c: Coefficient) -> Self {
        let mut terms = BTreeMap::new();
        if !c.is_zero() {
            terms.insert(e, c);
        }
        Self { terms }

    pub fn is_zero(&self) -> bool {
        self.terms.is_empty()
    }
    pub fn add(&self, o: &Self) -> Self {
        let mut terms = self.terms.clone();
        for (e, c) in &o.terms {
            let sum = terms.get(e).map_or_else(|| c.clone(), |p| p.add(c));
            if sum.is_zero() {
                terms.remove(e);
            } else {
                terms.insert(e.clone(), sum);
            }
        }
        Self { terms }

    /// Prepare a trace/observer value before publishing the step. A failed
    /// observer leaves the machine at the same prefix, including its history.
    pub fn step_once_observed<T>(
        &mut self,
        observe: impl FnOnce(&StepPoint, &ClassicalSnapshot, &ClassicalSnapshot) -> Result<T>,
    ) -> Result<Option<(StepPoint, T)>> {
        if !self.is_running() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let current = self.state.control.last().expect("running control").clone();
        let function = &self.object.functions[&current.function];
        let block = &function.blocks[current.block];
        let instruction = block
            .instructions
            .get(current.ip)
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::new("§3.4: fell off classical block".into()))?
            .clone();
        let point = StepPoint {
            function: current.function.clone(),
            block: block.name.clone(),
            index: current.ip,
            instruction,
        };
        let op = point.instruction.op.as_str();
        self.transaction(
            op == "uncall",
            matches!(op, "ret" | "uncall"),
            |trial, before| {
                trial.step_prepared()?;
                let value = observe(&point, before, &trial.state)?;
                Ok(Some((point, value)))
            },
        )
    }
    pub fn memory_bytes(&self, address: u128, len: usize) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
        self.transfer_bytes(address, len, "§7.1")
    }
    fn transfer_bytes(&self, address: u128, len: usize, rule: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
        if !self.state.memory.valid(address, len as u128) {
            return err("§7.1", "memory query outside live allocation");
        }
        let mut bytes = crate::reserved(len, rule, "§7.1 transfer bytes")?;
        bytes.extend((0..len).map(|n| self.state.memory.byte(address + n as u128)));
        Ok(bytes)
    }
    pub fn host_exchanges(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &HostExchange> {
        self.history.iter().filter_map(|h| h.host.as_ref())
    }
